0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eWith Guidance, you can create your own Guidance functions which can interact with language models.\nThese are marked using the \u003ccode\u003e@guidance\u003c/code\u003e decorator.\nSuppose we wanted to answer lots of multiple choice questions.\nWe could do something like the following:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"highlight highlight-source-python notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" dir=\"auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\"import guidance\n\nfrom guidance.models import Model\n\nASCII_OFFSET = ord(\u0026quot;a\u0026quot;)\n\n@guidance\ndef zero_shot_multiple_choice(\n    language_model: Model,\n    question: str,\n    choices: list[str],\n):\n    with user():\n        language_model += question + \u0026quot;\\n\u0026quot;\n        for i, choice in enumerate(choices):\n            language_model += f\u0026quot;{chr(i+ASCII_OFFSET)} : {choice}\\n\u0026quot;\n\n    with assistant():\n        language_model += select(\n            [chr(i + ASCII_OFFSET) for i in range(len(choices))], name=\u0026quot;string_choice\u0026quot;\n        )\n\n    return language_model\"\u003e\u003cpre\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-k\"\u003eimport\u003c/span\u003e \u003cspan dir=\"auto\"\u003eWhen running in a Jupyter Notebook so that the widget is active, we get the following output:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ca target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noopener noreferrer\" href=\"/guidance-ai/guidance/blob/main/docs/figures/widget_make_html_20250703.png\"\u003e\u003cimg src=\"/guidance-ai/guidance/raw/main/docs/figures/widget_make_html_20250703.png\" alt=\"Guidance widget showing HTML generation with token fast-forwarding\" style=\"max-width: 100%;\"\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eNote the varying highlighting of the generation.\nThis is showing another of Guidance's capabilities: fast-forwarding of tokens.\nThe constraints imposed by a grammar often mean that some tokens are known in advance.\nGuidance doesn't need the model to generate these; instead it can insert them into the generation.\nThis saves forward passes through the model, and hence reduces GPU usage.\nFor example, in the above HTML generation, Guidance always knows the last opening tag.\nIf the last opened tag was \u003ccode\u003e\u0026lt;h1\u0026gt;\u003c/code\u003e (for example), then as soon as the model generates \u003ccode\u003e\u0026lt;/\u003c/code\u003e, Guidance can fill in \u003ccode\u003eh1\u0026gt;\u003c/code\u003e without needing the model to perform a forward pass.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv using Guidance.\nThis is a common enough case that Guidance provides special support for it.\nA quick sample, based on a Pydantic model:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"highlight src=\"https://img.shields.io/badge/Hours-10am--2pm%20Pacific-gray\" alt=\"Hours\"\u003e","\u003c/div\u003e","\u003cdiv align=\"center\"\u003e\u003cpicture\u003e","  \u003csource media=\"(prefers-color-scheme: dark)\" srcset=\"docs/figures/guidance_logo_blue_dark.svg\"\u003e","  \u003cimg alt=\"guidance\" src=\"docs/figures/guidance_logo_blue.svg\" width=300\"\u003e","\u003c/picture\u003e\u003c/div\u003e","\u003cbr/\u003e","","**Guidance is an efficient programming paradigm for steering language models.** With Guidance, you can control how output is structured and get high-quality output for your use case—*while reducing latency and cost vs. conventional prompting or fine-tuning.* It allows users to constrain generation (e.g. with regex and CFGs) as well as to interleave control (conditionals, loops, tool use) and generation seamlessly.","","   * [Install](#install)","   * [Features](#features)","","","## Install","Guidance is available through PyPI and supports a variety of backends (Transformers, llama.cpp, OpenAI, etc.).","If you already have the backend required for your model, you can simply run","```bash","pip install guidance","```","","## Features","","### A Pythonic interface for language models","","When using Guidance, you can work with large language models using common Python idioms:","","```python","from guidance import system, user, assistant, gen","from guidance.models import Transformers","","# Could also do LlamaCpp or many other models","phi_lm = Transformers(\"microsoft/Phi-4-mini-instruct\")","","# Model objects are immutable, so this is a copy","lm = phi_lm","","with system():","    lm += \"You are a helpful assistant\"","","with user():","    lm += \"Hello. What is your name?\"","","with assistant():","    lm += gen(max_tokens=20)","","print(lm)","```","If run at the command line, this will produce output like:","```","\u003c|system|\u003eYou are a helpful assistant\u003c|end|\u003e\u003c|user|\u003eHello. What is your name?\u003c|end|\u003e\u003c|assistant|\u003eI am Phi, an AI developed by Microsoft. How can I help you today?","```","However, if running in a Jupyter notebook, then Guidance provides a widget for a richer user experience:","","\u003cimg src=\"docs/figures/widget_basic_example_20250703.png\" alt=\"Guidance widget showing HTML generation\" /\u003e","","With Guidance, it's really easy to capture generated text:","","```python","# Get a new copy of the Model","lm = phi_lm","","with system():","    lm += \"You are a helpful assistant\"","","with user():","    lm += \"Hello. What is your name?\"","","with assistant():","    lm += gen(name=\"lm_response\", max_tokens=20)","","print(f\"{lm['lm_response']=}\")","```","","```","lm['lm_response']='I am Phi, an AI developed by Microsoft. How can I help you today?'","```","","### Guarantee output syntax with constrained generation","","Guidance provides an easy to use, yet immensely powerful syntax for constraining the output of a language model.","For example, a `gen()` call can be constrained to match a regular expression:","","```python","lm = phi_lm","","with system():","    lm += \"You are a teenager\"","","with user():","    lm += \"How old are you?\"","","with assistant():","    lm += gen(\"lm_age\", regex=r\"\\d+\", temperature=0.8)","","print(f\"The language model is {lm['lm_age']} years old\")","```","","```","The language model is 13 years old","```","","Often, we know that the output has to be an item from a list we know in advance.","Guidance provides a `select()` function for this scenario:","","```python","from guidance import select","","lm = phi_lm","","with system():","    lm += \"You are a geography expert\"","","with user():","    lm += \"\"\"What is the capital of Sweden? Answer with the correct letter.","","    A) Helsinki","    B) Reykjavík ","    C) Stockholm","    D) Oslo","    \"\"\"","","with assistant():","    lm += select([\"A\", \"B\", \"C\", \"D\"], name=\"model_selection\")","","print(f\"The model selected {lm['model_selection']}\")","```","","```","The model selected C","```","","The constraint system offered by Guidance is extremely powerful.","It can ensure that the output conforms to any context free grammar (so long as the backend LLM has full support for Guidance).","More on this below.","","### Debug grammars offline (no model API calls)","","When iterating on constraints, you can validate candidate strings locally and test a full run with the `Mock` model.","","```python","from guidance import gen","from guidance.models import Mock","","grammar = \"expr=\" + gen(regex=r\"\\d+([+*]\\d+)*\", name=\"expr\")","","# 1) Validate strings directly against the grammar","assert grammar.match(\"expr=12+7*3\") is not None","assert grammar.match(\"expr=12+*3\") is None","","# 2) Run the same grammar with a local mock model","lm = Mock(b\"\u003cs\u003eexpr=12+7*3\")","lm += grammar","print(lm[\"expr\"])  # 12+7*3","```","","### Create your own Guidance functions","","With Guidance, you can create your own Guidance functions which can interact with language models.","These are marked using the `@guidance` decorator.","Suppose we wanted to answer lots of multiple choice questions.","We could do something like the following:","","```python","import guidance","","from guidance.models import Model","","ASCII_OFFSET = ord(\"a\")","","@guidance","def zero_shot_multiple_choice(","    language_model: Model,","    question: str,","    choices: list[str],","):","    with user():","        language_model += question + \"\\n\"","        for i, choice in enumerate(choices):","            language_model += f\"{chr(i+ASCII_OFFSET)} : {choice}\\n\"","","    with assistant():","        language_model += select(","            [chr(i + ASCII_OFFSET) for i in range(len(choices))], name=\"string_choice\"","        )","","    return language_model","```","Now, define some questions:","```python","questions = [","    {","        \"question\" : \"Which state has the northernmost capital?\",","        \"choices\" : [","            \"New South Wales\",","            \"Northern Territory\",","            \"Queensland\",","            \"South Australia\",","            \"Tasmania\",","            \"Victoria\",","            \"Western Australia\",","        ],","        \"answer\" : 1,","    },","    {","        \"question\" : \"Which of the following is venomous?\",","        \"choices\" : [","            \"Kangaroo\",","            \"Koala Bear\",","            \"Platypus\",","        ],","        \"answer\" : 2,","    }","]","```","We can use our decorated function like `gen()` or `select()`.","The `language_model` argument will be filled in for us automatically:","```python","lm = phi_lm","","with system():","    lm += \"You are a student taking a multiple choice test.\"","","for mcq in questions:","    lm_temp = lm + zero_shot_multiple_choice(question=mcq[\"question\"], choices=mcq[\"choices\"])","    converted_answer = ord(lm_temp[\"string_choice\"]) - ASCII_OFFSET","    print(lm_temp)","    print(f\"LM Answer: {converted_answer},  Correct Answer: {mcq['answer']}\")","```","","```","\u003c|system|\u003eYou are a student taking a multiple choice test.\u003c|end|\u003e\u003c|user|\u003eWhich state has the northernmost capital?","a : New South Wales","b : Northern Territory","c : Queensland","d : South Australia","e : Tasmania","f : Victoria","g : Western Australia","\u003c|end|\u003e\u003c|assistant|\u003eb","LM Answer: 1,  Correct Answer: 1","\u003c|system|\u003eYou are a student taking a multiple choice test.\u003c|end|\u003e\u003c|user|\u003eWhich of the following is venomous?","a : Kangaroo","b : Koala Bear","c : Platypus","\u003c|end|\u003e\u003c|assistant|\u003ec","LM Answer: 2,  Correct Answer: 2","```","","Guidance functions can be composed, in order to construct a full context free grammar.","For example, we can create Guidance functions to build a simple HTML webpage (note that this is _not_ a full implementation of HTML).","We start with a simple function which will generate text which does not contain any HTML tags.","The function is marked as `stateless` to indicate that we intend to use it for composing a grammar:","","```python","@guidance(stateless=True)","def _gen_text(lm: Model):","    return lm + gen(regex=\"[^\u003c\u003e]+\") ","```","","We can then use this function to generate text within an arbitrary HTML tag:","```python","@guidance(stateless=True)","def _gen_text_in_tag(lm: Model, tag: str):","    lm += f\"\u003c{tag}\u003e\"","    lm += _gen_text()","    lm += f\"\u003c/{tag}\u003e\"","    return lm","```","Now, let us create the page header. As part of this, we need to generate a page title:","```python","@guidance(stateless=True)","def _gen_header(lm: Model):","    lm += \"\u003chead\u003e\\n\"","    lm += _gen_text_in_tag(\"title\") + \"\\n\"","    lm += \"\u003c/head\u003e\\n\"","    return lm","```","The body of the HTML page is going to be filled with headings and paragraphs.","We can define a function to do each:","```python","from guidance.library import one_or_more","","@guidance(stateless=True)","def _gen_heading(lm: Model):","    lm += select(","        options=[_gen_text_in_tag(\"h1\"), _gen_text_in_tag(\"h2\"), _gen_text_in_tag(\"h3\")]","    )","    lm += \"\\n\"","    return lm","","@guidance(stateless=True)","def _gen_para(lm: Model):","    lm += \"\u003cp\u003e\"","    lm += one_or_more(","        select(","            options=[","                _gen_text(),","                _gen_text_in_tag(\"em\"),","                _gen_text_in_tag(\"strong\"),","                \"\u003cbr /\u003e\",","            ],","        )","    )","    lm += \"\u003c/p\u003e\\n\"","    return lm","```","Now, the function to define the body of the HTML itself:","```python","@guidance(stateless=True)","def _gen_body(lm: Model):","    lm += \"\u003cbody\u003e\\n\"","    lm += one_or_more(select(options=[_gen_heading(), one_or_more(_gen_para())]))","    lm += \"\u003c/body\u003e\\n\"","    return lm","```","Next, we come to the function which generates the complete HTML page.","We add the HTML start tag, then generate the header, then body, and then append the ending HTML tag:","```python","@guidance(stateless=True)","def _gen_html(lm: Model):","    lm += \"\u003chtml\u003e\\n\"","    lm += _gen_header()","    lm += _gen_body()","    lm += \"\u003c/html\u003e\\n\"","    return lm","```","Finally, we provide a user-friendly wrapper, which will allow us to:","- Set the temperature of the generation","- Capture the generated page from the Model object","```python","from guidance.library import capture, with_temperature","","@guidance(stateless=True)","def make_html(","    lm,","    name: str | None = None,","    *,","    temperature: float = 0.0,","):","    return lm + capture(","        with_temperature(_gen_html(), temperature=temperature),","        name=name,","    )","```","Now, use this to generate a simple webpage:","```python","lm = phi_lm","","with system():","    lm += \"You are an expert in HTML\"","","with user():","    lm += \"Create a simple and short web page about your life story.\"","","with assistant():","    lm += make_html(name=\"html_text\", temperature=0.7)","```","","When running in a Jupyter Notebook so that the widget is active, we get the following output:","","\u003cimg src=\"docs/figures/widget_make_html_20250703.png\" alt=\"Guidance widget showing HTML generation with token fast-forwarding\" /\u003e","","Note the varying highlighting of the generation.","This is showing another of Guidance's capabilities: fast-forwarding of tokens.","The constraints imposed by a grammar often mean that some tokens are known in advance.","Guidance doesn't need the model to generate these; instead it can insert them into the generation.","This saves forward passes through the model, and hence reduces GPU usage.","For example, in the above HTML generation, Guidance always knows the last opening tag.","If the last opened tag was `\u003ch1\u003e` (for example), then as soon as the model generates `\u003c/`, Guidance can fill in `h1\u003e` without needing the model to perform a forward pass.","","### Generating JSON","","A JSON schema is actually a context free grammar, and hence it can be used to constrain an LLM using Guidance.","This is a common enough case that Guidance provides special support for it.","A quick sample, based on a Pydantic model:","```python","import json","from pydantic import BaseModel, Field","","from guidance import json as gen_json","","class BloodPressure(BaseModel):","    systolic: int = Field(gt=300, le=400)","    diastolic: int = Field(gt=0, le=20)","    location: str = Field(max_length=50)","    model_config = dict(extra=\"forbid\")","","lm = phi_lm","","with system():","    lm += \"You are a doctor taking a patient's blood pressure taken from their arm\"","","with user():","    lm += \"Report the blood pressure\"","","with assistant():","    lm += gen_json(name=\"bp\", schema=BloodPressure)","","print(f\"{lm['bp']=}\")","","# Use Python's JSON library","loaded_json = json.loads(lm[\"bp\"])","print(json.dumps(loaded_json, indent=4))","","# Use Pydantic","result = BloodPressure.model_validate_json(lm[\"bp\"])","print(result.model_dump_json(indent=8))","```","```","lm['bp']='{\"systolic\": 301, \"diastolic\": 15, \"location\": \"arm\"}'","{","    \"systolic\": 301,","    \"diastolic\": 15,","    \"location\": \"arm\"","}","{","        \"systolic\": 301,","        \"diastolic\": 15,","        \"location\": \"arm\"","}","```","Note that the generated blood pressure is not one the model will have seen for a human.","When generating JSON, a substantial number of tokens can often be fast-forwarded, due to the structural constraints imposed by the schema."],"stylingDirectives":[[[1,4,"pl-ent"],[5,10,"pl-e"],[11,12,"pl-s"],[12,17,"pl-s"],[17,18,"pl-s"]],[[3,4,"pl-ent"],[5,9,"pl-e"],[10,11,"pl-s"],[11,40,"pl-s"],[40,41,"pl-s"],[43,46,"pl-ent"],[47,50,"pl-e"],[51,52,"pl-s"],[52,134,"pl-s"],[134,135,"pl-s"],[136,139,"pl-e"],[140,141,"pl-s"],[141,148,"pl-s"],[148,149,"pl-s"],[152,153,"pl-ent"]],[[3,4,"pl-ent"],[5,9,"pl-e"],[10,11,"pl-s"],[11,42,"pl-s"],[42,43,"pl-s"],[45,48,"pl-ent"],[49,52,"pl-e"],[53,54,"pl-s"],[54,161,"pl-s"],[161,162,"pl-s"],[163,166,"pl-e"],[167,168,"pl-s"],[168,173,"pl-s"],[173,174,"pl-s"],[177,178,"pl-ent"]],[[3,6,"pl-ent"],[7,10,"pl-e"],[11,12,"pl-s"],[12,71,"pl-s"],[71,72,"pl-s"],[73,76,"pl-e"],[77,78,"pl-s"],[78,83,"pl-s"],[83,84,"pl-s"]],[[2,5,"pl-ent"]],[[1,4,"pl-ent"],[5,10,"pl-e"],[11,12,"pl-s"],[12,18,"pl-s"],[18,19,"pl-s"],[21,28,"pl-ent"]],[[3,9,"pl-ent"],[10,15,"pl-e"],[16,17,"pl-s"],[17,45,"pl-s"],[45,46,"pl-s"],[47,53,"pl-e"],[54,55,"pl-s"],[55,95,"pl-s"],[95,96,"pl-s"]],[],[[2,9,"pl-ent"],[12,15,"pl-ent"]],[[1,3,"pl-ent"]],[],[[0,2,"pl-s"],[77,79,"pl-s"],[182,183,"pl-s"],[257,258,"pl-s"]],[],[[3,4,"pl-v"],[5,6,"pl-s"],[13,14,"pl-s"],[14,15,"pl-s"],[15,23,"pl-corl"],[23,24,"pl-s"]],[[3,4,"pl-v"],[5,6,"pl-s"],[14,15,"pl-s"],[15,16,"pl-s"],[16,25,"pl-corl"],[25,26,"pl-s"]],[],[],[[0,10,"pl-mh"],[3,10,"pl-en"]],[],[],[[0,3,"pl-s"],[3,7,"pl-en"]],[],[[0,3,"pl-s"]],[],[[0,11,"pl-mh"],[3,11,"pl-en"]],[],[[0,44,"pl-mh"],[4,44,"pl-en"]],[],[],[],[[0,3,"pl-s"],[3,9,"pl-en"]],[[0,4,"pl-k"],[14,20,"pl-k"]],[[0,4,"pl-k"],[21,27,"pl-k"]],[],[[0,45,"pl-c"],[0,1,"pl-c"]],[[7,8,"pl-k"],[22,53,"pl-s"],[22,23,"pl-pds"],[52,53,"pl-pds"]],[],[[0,48,"pl-c"],[0,1,"pl-c"]],[[3,4,"pl-k"]],[],[[0,4,"pl-k"]],[[7,9,"pl-k"],[10,39,"pl-s"],[10,11,"pl-pds"],[38,39,"pl-pds"]],[],[[0,4,"pl-k"]],[[7,9,"pl-k"],[10,37,"pl-s"],[10,11,"pl-pds"],[36,37,"pl-pds"]],[],[[0,4,"pl-k"]],[[7,9,"pl-k"],[14,24,"pl-v"],[24,25,"pl-k"],[25,27,"pl-c1"]],[],[[0,5,"pl-c1"]],[[0,3,"pl-s"]],[],[[0,3,"pl-s"],[3,3,"pl-c1"]],[[0,162," <div align="center" dir="auto"><themed-picture data-catalyst-inline="true"><picture>
  <source media="(prefers-color-scheme: dark)" srcset="/guidance-ai/guidance/raw/main/docs/figures/guidance_logo_blue_dark.svg">
  <img alt="guidance" src="/guidance-ai/guidance/raw/main/docs/figures/guidance_logo_blue.svg" width="300&quot;">
</picture></themed-picture></div>
<br>
<p dir="auto"><strong>Guidance is an efficient programming paradigm for steering language models.</strong> With Guidance, you can control how output is structured and get high-quality output for your use case—<em>while reducing latency and cost vs. conventional prompting or fine-tuning.</em> It allows users to constrain generation (e.g. with regex and CFGs) as well as to interleave control (conditionals, loops, tool use) and generation seamlessly.</p>
<ul dir="auto">
<li><a href="#install">Install</a></li>
<li><a href="#features">Features</a></li>
</ul>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h2 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Install</h2><a id="user-content-install" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Install" href="#install"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">Guidance is available through PyPI and supports a variety of backends (Transformers, llama.cpp, OpenAI, etc.).
If you already have the backend required for your model, you can simply run</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-shell notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" dir="auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="pip install guidance"><pre>pip install guidance</pre></div>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h2 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Features</h2><a id="user-content-features" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Features" href="#features"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">A Pythonic interface for language models</h3><a id="user-content-a-pythonic-interface-for-language-models" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: A Pythonic interface for language models" href="#a-pythonic-interface-for-language-models"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">When using Guidance, you can work with large language models using common Python idioms:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-python notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" dir="auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="from guidance import system, user, assistant, gen
from guidance.models import Transformers

# Could also do LlamaCpp or many other models
phi_lm = Transformers(&quot;microsoft/Phi-4-mini-instruct&quot;)

# Model objects are immutable, so this is a copy
lm = phi_lm

with system():
    lm += &quot;You are a helpful assistant&quot;

with user():
    lm += &quot;Hello. What is your name?&quot;

with assistant():
    lm += gen(max_tokens=20)

print(lm)"><pre><span class="pl-k">from</span> <span class="pl-s1">guidance</span> <span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-s1">system</span>, <span class="pl-s1">user</span>, <span class="pl-s1">assistant</span>, <span class="pl-s1">gen</span>
<span class="pl-k">from</span> <span class="pl-s1">guidance</span>.<span class="pl-s1">models</span> <span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-v">Transformers</span>

<span class="pl-c"># Could also do LlamaCpp or many other models</span>
<span class="pl-s1">phi_lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-en">Transformers</span>(<span class="pl-s">"microsoft/Phi-4-mini-instruct"</span>)

<span class="pl-c"># Model objects are immutable, so this is a copy</span>
<span class="pl-s1">lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">phi_lm</span>

<span class="pl-k">with</span> <span class="pl-en">system</span>():
    <span class="pl-s1">lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">+=</span> <span class="pl-s">"You are a helpful assistant"</span>

<span class="pl-k">with</span> <span class="pl-en">user</span>():
    <span class="pl-s1">lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">+=</span> <span class="pl-s">"Hello. What is your name?"</span>

<span class="pl-k">with</span> <span class="pl-en">assistant</span>():
    <span class="pl-s1">lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">+=</span> <span class="pl-en">gen</span>(<span class="pl-s1">max_tokens</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-c1">20</span>)

<span class="pl-en">print</span>(<span class="pl-s1">lm</span>)</pre></div>
<p dir="auto">If run at the command line, this will produce output like:</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="&lt;|system|&gt;You are a helpful assistant&lt;|end|&gt;&lt;|user|&gt;Hello. What is your name?&lt;|end|&gt;&lt;|assistant|&gt;I am Phi, an AI developed by Microsoft. How can I help you today?"><pre class="notranslate"><code>&lt;|system|&gt;You are a helpful assistant&lt;|end|&gt;&lt;|user|&gt;Hello. What is your name?&lt;|end|&gt;&lt;|assistant|&gt;I am Phi, an AI developed by Microsoft. How can I help you today?
</code></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">However, if running in a Jupyter notebook, then Guidance provides a widget for a richer user experience:</p>
<p dir="auto"><a target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer" href="/guidance-ai/guidance/blob/main/docs/figures/widget_basic_example_20250703.png"><img src="/guidance-ai/guidance/raw/main/docs/figures/widget_basic_example_20250703.png" alt="Guidance widget showing HTML generation" style="max-width: 100%;"></a></p>
<p dir="auto">With Guidance, it's really easy to capture generated text:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-python notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" dir="auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="# Get a new copy of the Model
lm = phi_lm

with system():
    lm += &quot;You are a helpful assistant&quot;

with user():
    lm += &quot;Hello. What is your name?&quot;

with assistant():
    lm += gen(name=&quot;lm_response&quot;, max_tokens=20)

print(f&quot;{lm['lm_response']=}&quot;)"><pre><span class="pl-c"># Get a new copy of the Model</span>
<span class="pl-s1">lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">phi_lm</span>

<span class="pl-k">with</span> <span class="pl-en">system</span>():
    <span class="pl-s1">lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">+=</span> <span class="pl-s">"You are a helpful assistant"</span>

<span class="pl-k">with</span> <span class="pl-en">user</span>():
    <span class="pl-s1">lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">+=</span> <span class="pl-s">"Hello. What is your name?"</span>

<span class="pl-k">with</span> <span class="pl-en">assistant</span>():
    <span class="pl-s1">lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">+=</span> <span class="pl-en">gen</span>(<span class="pl-s1">name</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-s">"lm_response"</span>, <span class="pl-s1">max_tokens</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-c1">20</span>)

<span class="pl-en">print</span>(<span class="pl-s">f"<span class="pl-s1"><span class="pl-kos">{</span><span class="pl-s1">lm</span>[<span class="pl-s">'lm_response'</span>]<span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-kos">}</span></span>"</span>)</pre></div>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="lm['lm_response']='I am Phi, an AI developed by Microsoft. How can I help you today?'"><pre class="notranslate"><code>lm['lm_response']='I am Phi, an AI developed by Microsoft. How can I help you today?'
</code></pre></div>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Guarantee output syntax with constrained generation</h3><a id="user-content-guarantee-output-syntax-with-constrained-generation" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Guarantee output syntax with constrained generation" href="#guarantee-output-syntax-with-constrained-generation"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">Guidance provides an easy to use, yet immensely powerful syntax for constraining the output of a language model.
For example, a <code>gen()</code> call can be constrained to match a regular expression:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-python notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" dir="auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="lm = phi_lm

with system():
    lm += &quot;You are a teenager&quot;

with user():
    lm += &quot;How old are you?&quot;

with assistant():
    lm += gen(&quot;lm_age&quot;, regex=r&quot;\d+&quot;, temperature=0.8)

print(f&quot;The language model is {lm['lm_age']} years old&quot;)"><pre><span class="pl-s1">lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">phi_lm</span>

<span class="pl-k">with</span> <span class="pl-en">system</span>():
    <span class="pl-s1">lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">+=</span> <span class="pl-s">"You are a teenager"</span>

<span class="pl-k">with</span> <span class="pl-en">user</span>():
    <span class="pl-s1">lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">+=</span> <span class="pl-s">"How old are you?"</span>

<span class="pl-k">with</span> <span class="pl-en">assistant</span>():
    <span class="pl-s1">lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">+=</span> <span class="pl-en">gen</span>(<span class="pl-s">"lm_age"</span>, <span class="pl-s1">regex</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-s">r"\d+"</span>, <span class="pl-s1">temperature</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-c1">0.8</span>)

<span class="pl-en">print</span>(<span class="pl-s">f"The language model is <span class="pl-s1"><span class="pl-kos">{</span><span class="pl-s1">lm</span>[<span class="pl-s">'lm_age'</span>]<span class="pl-kos">}</span></span> years old"</span>)</pre></div>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="The language model is 13 years old"><pre class="notranslate"><code>The language model is 13 years old
</code></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">Often, we know that the output has to be an item from a list we know in advance.
Guidance provides a <code>select()</code> function for this scenario:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-python notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" dir="auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="from guidance import select

lm = phi_lm

with system():
    lm += &quot;You are a geography expert&quot;

with user():
    lm += &quot;&quot;&quot;What is the capital of Sweden? Answer with the correct letter.

    A) Helsinki
    B) Reykjavík 
    C) Stockholm
    D) Oslo
    &quot;&quot;&quot;

with assistant():
    lm += select([&quot;A&quot;, &quot;B&quot;, &quot;C&quot;, &quot;D&quot;], name=&quot;model_selection&quot;)

print(f&quot;The model selected {lm['model_selection']}&quot;)"><pre><span class="pl-k">from</span> <span class="pl-s1">guidance</span> <span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-s1">select</span>

<span class="pl-s1">lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">phi_lm</span>

<span class="pl-k">with</span> <span class="pl-en">system</span>():
    <span class="pl-s1">lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">+=</span> <span class="pl-s">"You are a geography expert"</span>

<span class="pl-k">with</span> <span class="pl-en">user</span>():
    <span class="pl-s1">lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">+=</span> <span class="pl-s">"""What is the capital of Sweden? Answer with the correct letter.</span>
<span class="pl-s"></span>
<span class="pl-s">    A) Helsinki</span>
<span class="pl-s">    B) Reykjavík </span>
<span class="pl-s">    C) Stockholm</span>
<span class="pl-s">    D) Oslo</span>
<span class="pl-s">    """</span>

<span class="pl-k">with</span> <span class="pl-en">assistant</span>():
    <span class="pl-s1">lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">+=</span> <span class="pl-en">select</span>([<span class="pl-s">"A"</span>, <span class="pl-s">"B"</span>, <span class="pl-s">"C"</span>, <span class="pl-s">"D"</span>], <span class="pl-s1">name</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-s">"model_selection"</span>)

<span class="pl-en">print</span>(<span class="pl-s">f"The model selected <span class="pl-s1"><span class="pl-kos">{</span><span class="pl-s1">lm</span>[<span class="pl-s">'model_selection'</span>]<span class="pl-kos">}</span></span>"</span>)</pre></div>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="The model selected C"><pre class="notranslate"><code>The model selected C
</code></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">The constraint system offered by Guidance is extremely powerful.
It can ensure that the output conforms to any context free grammar (so long as the backend LLM has full support for Guidance).
More on this below.</p>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Debug grammars offline (no model API calls)</h3><a id="user-content-debug-grammars-offline-no-model-api-calls" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Debug grammars offline (no model API calls)" href="#debug-grammars-offline-no-model-api-calls"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">When iterating on constraints, you can validate candidate strings locally and test a full run with the <code>Mock</code> model.</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-python notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" dir="auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="from guidance import gen
from guidance.models import Mock

grammar = &quot;expr=&quot; + gen(regex=r&quot;\d+([+*]\d+)*&quot;, name=&quot;expr&quot;)

# 1) Validate strings directly against the grammar
assert grammar.match(&quot;expr=12+7*3&quot;) is not None
assert grammar.match(&quot;expr=12+*3&quot;) is None

# 2) Run the same grammar with a local mock model
lm = Mock(b&quot;&lt;s&gt;expr=12+7*3&quot;)
lm += grammar
print(lm[&quot;expr&quot;])  # 12+7*3"><pre><span class="pl-k">from</span> <span class="pl-s1">guidance</span> <span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-s1">gen</span>
<span class="pl-k">from</span> <span class="pl-s1">guidance</span>.<span class="pl-s1">models</span> <span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-v">Mock</span>

<span class="pl-s1">grammar</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s">"expr="</span> <span class="pl-c1">+</span> <span class="pl-en">gen</span>(<span class="pl-s1">regex</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-s">r"\d+([+*]\d+)*"</span>, <span class="pl-s1">name</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-s">"expr"</span>)

<span class="pl-c"># 1) Validate strings directly against the grammar</span>
<span class="pl-k">assert</span> <span class="pl-s1">grammar</span>.<span class="pl-c1">match</span>(<span class="pl-s">"expr=12+7*3"</span>) <span class="pl-c1"><span class="pl-c1">is</span> <span class="pl-c1">not</span></span> <span class="pl-c1">None</span>
<span class="pl-k">assert</span> <span class="pl-s1">grammar</span>.<span class="pl-c1">match</span>(<span class="pl-s">"expr=12+*3"</span>) <span class="pl-c1">is</span> <span class="pl-c1">None</span>

<span class="pl-c"># 2) Run the same grammar with a local mock model</span>
<span class="pl-s1">lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-en">Mock</span>(<span class="pl-s">b"&lt;s&gt;expr=12+7*3"</span>)
<span class="pl-s1">lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">+=</span> <span class="pl-s1">grammar</span>
<span class="pl-en">print</span>(<span class="pl-s1">lm</span>[<span class="pl-s">"expr"</span>])  <span class="pl-c"># 12+7*3</span></pre></div>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Create your own Guidance functions</h3><a id="user-content-create-your-own-guidance-functions" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Create your own Guidance functions" href="#create-your-own-guidance-functions"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">With Guidance, you can create your own Guidance functions which can interact with language models.
These are marked using the <code>@guidance</code> decorator.
Suppose we wanted to answer lots of multiple choice questions.
We could do something like the following:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-python notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" dir="auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="import guidance

from guidance.models import Model

ASCII_OFFSET = ord(&quot;a&quot;)

@guidance
def zero_shot_multiple_choice(
    language_model: Model,
    question: str,
    choices: list[str],
):
    with user():
        language_model += question + &quot;\n&quot;
        for i, choice in enumerate(choices):
            language_model += f&quot;{chr(i+ASCII_OFFSET)} : {choice}\n&quot;

    with assistant():
        language_model += select(
            [chr(i + ASCII_OFFSET) for i in range(len(choices))], name=&quot;string_choice&quot;
        )

    return language_model"><pre><span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-s1">guidance</span>

<span class="pl-k">from</span> <span class="pl-s1">guidance</span>.<span class="pl-s1">models</span> <span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-v">Model</span>

<span class="pl-c1">ASCII_OFFSET</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-en">ord</span>(<span class="pl-s">"a"</span>)

<span class="pl-en">@<span class="pl-s1">guidance</span></span>
<span class="pl-k">def</span> <span class="pl-en">zero_shot_multiple_choice</span>(
    <span class="pl-s1">language_model</span>: <span class="pl-smi">Model</span>,
    <span class="pl-s1">question</span>: <span class="pl-smi">str</span>,
    <span class="pl-s1">choices</span>: <span class="pl-s1">list</span>[<span class="pl-smi">str</span>],
):
    <span class="pl-k">with</span> <span class="pl-en">user</span>():
        <span class="pl-s1">language_model</span> <span class="pl-c1">+=</span> <span class="pl-s1">question</span> <span class="pl-c1">+</span> <span class="pl-s">"<span class="pl-cce">\n</span>"</span>
        <span class="pl-k">for</span> <span class="pl-s1">i</span>, <span class="pl-s1">choice</span> <span class="pl-c1">in</span> <span class="pl-en">enumerate</span>(<span class="pl-s1">choices</span>):
            <span class="pl-s1">language_model</span> <span class="pl-c1">+=</span> <span class="pl-s">f"<span class="pl-s1"><span class="pl-kos">{</span><span class="pl-en">chr</span>(<span class="pl-s1">i</span><span class="pl-c1">+</span><span class="pl-c1">ASCII_OFFSET</span>)<span class="pl-kos">}</span></span> : <span class="pl-s1"><span class="pl-kos">{</span><span class="pl-s1">choice</span><span class="pl-kos">}</span></span><span class="pl-cce">\n</span>"</span>

    <span class="pl-k">with</span> <span class="pl-en">assistant</span>():
        <span class="pl-s1">language_model</span> <span class="pl-c1">+=</span> <span class="pl-en">select</span>(
            [<span class="pl-en">chr</span>(<span class="pl-s1">i</span> <span class="pl-c1">+</span> <span class="pl-c1">ASCII_OFFSET</span>) <span class="pl-k">for</span> <span class="pl-s1">i</span> <span class="pl-c1">in</span> <span class="pl-en">range</span>(<span class="pl-en">len</span>(<span class="pl-s1">choices</span>))], <span class="pl-s1">name</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-s">"string_choice"</span>
        )

    <span class="pl-k">return</span> <span class="pl-s1">language_model</span></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">Now, define some questions:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-python notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" dir="auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="questions = [
    {
        &quot;question&quot; : &quot;Which state has the northernmost capital?&quot;,
        &quot;choices&quot; : [
            &quot;New South Wales&quot;,
            &quot;Northern Territory&quot;,
            &quot;Queensland&quot;,
            &quot;South Australia&quot;,
            &quot;Tasmania&quot;,
            &quot;Victoria&quot;,
            &quot;Western Australia&quot;,
        ],
        &quot;answer&quot; : 1,
    },
    {
        &quot;question&quot; : &quot;Which of the following is venomous?&quot;,
        &quot;choices&quot; : [
            &quot;Kangaroo&quot;,
            &quot;Koala Bear&quot;,
            &quot;Platypus&quot;,
        ],
        &quot;answer&quot; : 2,
    }
]"><pre><span class="pl-s1">questions</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> [
    {
        <span class="pl-s">"question"</span> : <span class="pl-s">"Which state has the northernmost capital?"</span>,
        <span class="pl-s">"choices"</span> : [
            <span class="pl-s">"New South Wales"</span>,
            <span class="pl-s">"Northern Territory"</span>,
            <span class="pl-s">"Queensland"</span>,
            <span class="pl-s">"South Australia"</span>,
            <span class="pl-s">"Tasmania"</span>,
            <span class="pl-s">"Victoria"</span>,
            <span class="pl-s">"Western Australia"</span>,
        ],
        <span class="pl-s">"answer"</span> : <span class="pl-c1">1</span>,
    },
    {
        <span class="pl-s">"question"</span> : <span class="pl-s">"Which of the following is venomous?"</span>,
        <span class="pl-s">"choices"</span> : [
            <span class="pl-s">"Kangaroo"</span>,
            <span class="pl-s">"Koala Bear"</span>,
            <span class="pl-s">"Platypus"</span>,

    }
]</pre></div>
<p dir="auto">We can use our decorated function like <code>gen()</code> or <code>select()</code>.
The <code>language_model</code> argument will be filled in for us automatically:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-python notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" dir="auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="lm = phi_lm

with system():
    lm += &quot;You are a student taking a multiple choice test.&quot;

for mcq in questions:
    lm_temp = lm + zero_shot_multiple_choice(question=mcq[&quot;question&quot;], choices=mcq[&quot;choices&quot;])
    converted_answer = ord(lm_temp[&quot;string_choice&quot;]) - ASCII_OFFSET
    print(lm_temp)
    print(f&quot;LM Answer: {converted_answer},  Correct Answer: {mcq['answer']}&quot;)"><pre><span class="pl-s1">lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">phi_lm</span>

<span class="pl-k">with</span> <span class="pl-en">system</span>():
    <span class="pl-s1">lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">+=</span> <span class="pl-s">"You are a student taking a multiple choice test."</span>

<span class="pl-k">for</span> <span class="pl-s1">mcq</span> <span class="pl-c1">in</span> <span class="pl-s1">questions</span>:
    <span class="pl-s1">lm_temp</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">+</span> <span class="pl-en">zero_shot_multiple_choice</span>(<span class="pl-s1">question</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-s1">mcq</span>[<span class="pl-s">"question"</span>], <span class="pl-s1">choices</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-s1">mcq</span>[<span class="pl-s">"choices"</span>])
    <span class="pl-s1">converted_answer</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-en">ord</span>(<span class="pl-s1">lm_temp</span>[<span class="pl-s">"string_choice"</span>]) <span class="pl-c1">-</span> <span class="pl-c1">ASCII_OFFSET</span>
    <span class="pl-en">print</span>(<span class="pl-s1">lm_temp</span>)
    <span class="pl-en">print</span>(<span class="pl-s">f"LM Answer: <span class="pl-s1"><span class="pl-kos">{</span><span class="pl-s1">converted_answer</span><span class="pl-kos">}</span></span>,  Correct Answer: <span class="pl-s1"><span class="pl-kos">{</span><span class="pl-s1">mcq</span>[<span class="pl-s">'answer'</span>]<span class="pl-kos">}</span></span>"</span>)</pre></div>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="&lt;|system|&gt;You are a student taking a multiple choice test.&lt;|end|&gt;&lt;|user|&gt;Which state has the northernmost capital?

<p dir="auto">Guidance functions can be composed, in order to construct a full context free grammar.
For example, we can create Guidance functions to build a simple HTML webpage (note that this is <em>not</em> a full implementation of HTML).
We start with a simple function which will generate text which does not contain any HTML tags.
The function is marked as <code>stateless</code> to indicate that we intend to use it for composing a grammar:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-python notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" dir="auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="@guidance(stateless=True)
def _gen_text(lm: Model):
    return lm + gen(regex=&quot;[^&lt;&gt;]+&quot;) "><pre><span class="pl-en">@<span class="pl-en">guidance</span>(<span class="pl-s1">stateless</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-c1">True</span>)</span>
<span class="pl-k">def</span> <span class="pl-en">_gen_text</span>(<span class="pl-s1">lm</span>: <span class="pl-smi">Model</span>):
    <span class="pl-k">return</span> <span class="pl-s1">lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">+</span> <span class="pl-en">gen</span>(<span class="pl-s1">regex</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-s">"[^&lt;&gt;]+"</span>) </pre></div>
<p dir="auto">We can then use this function to generate text within an arbitrary HTML tag:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-python notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" dir="auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="@guidance(stateless=True)
def _gen_text_in_tag(lm: Model, tag: str):
    lm += f&quot;&lt;{tag}&gt;&quot;
    lm += _gen_text()
    lm += f&quot;&lt;/{tag}&gt;&quot;
    return lm"><pre><span class="pl-en">@<span class="pl-en">guidance</span>(<span class="pl-s1">stateless</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-c1">True</span>)</span>
<span class="pl-k">def</span> <span class="pl-en">_gen_text_in_tag</span>(<span class="pl-s1">lm</span>: <span class="pl-smi">Model</span>, <span class="pl-s1">tag</span>: <span class="pl-smi">str</span>):
    <span class="pl-s1">lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">+=</span> <span class="pl-s">f"&lt;<span class="pl-s1"><span class="pl-kos">{</span><span class="pl-s1">tag</span><span class="pl-kos">}</span></span>&gt;"</span>
    <span class="pl-s1">lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">+=</span> <span class="pl-en">_gen_text</span>()
    <span class="pl-s1">lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">+=</span> <span class="pl-s">f"&lt;/<span class="pl-s1"><span class="pl-kos">{</span><span class="pl-s1">tag</span><span class="pl-kos">}</span></span>&gt;"</span>
    <span class="pl-k">return</span> <span class="pl-s1">lm</span></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">Now, let us create the page header. As part of this, we need to generate a page title:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-python notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" dir="auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="@guidance(stateless=True)
def _gen_header(lm: Model):
    lm += &quot;&lt;head&gt;\n&quot;
    lm += _gen_text_in_tag(&quot;title&quot;) + &quot;\n&quot;
    lm += &quot;&lt;/head&gt;\n&quot;
    return lm"><pre><span class="pl-en">@<span class="pl-en">guidance</span>(<span class="pl-s1">stateless</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-c1">True</span>)</span>
<span class="pl-k">def</span> <span class="pl-en">_gen_header</span>(<span class="pl-s1">lm</span>: <span class="pl-smi">Model</span>):
    <span class="pl-s1">lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">+=</span> <span class="pl-s">"&lt;head&gt;<span class="pl-cce">\n</span>"</span>
    <span class="pl-s1">lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">+=</span> <span class="pl-en">_gen_text_in_tag</span>(<span class="pl-s">"title"</span>) <span class="pl-c1">+</span> <span class="pl-s">"<span class="pl-cce">\n</span>"</span>
    <span class="pl-s1">lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">+=</span> <span class="pl-s">"&lt;/head&gt;<span class="pl-cce">\n</span>"</span>
    <span class="pl-k">return</span> <span class="pl-s1">lm</span></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">The body of the HTML page is going to be filled with headings and paragraphs.
We can define a function to do each:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-python notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" dir="auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="from guidance.library import one_or_more

@guidance(stateless=True)
def _gen_heading(lm: Model):
    lm += select(
        options=[_gen_text_in_tag(&quot;h1&quot;), _gen_text_in_tag(&quot;h2&quot;), _gen_text_in_tag(&quot;h3&quot;)]
    )
    lm += &quot;\n&quot;
    return lm

@guidance(stateless=True)
def _gen_para(lm: Model):
    lm += &quot;&lt;p&gt;&quot;
    lm += one_or_more(
        select(
            options=[
                _gen_text(),
                _gen_text_in_tag(&quot;em&quot;),
                _gen_text_in_tag(&quot;strong&quot;),
                &quot;&lt;br /&gt;&quot;,
            ],
        )
    )
    lm += &quot;&lt;/p&gt;\n&quot;
    return lm"><pre><span class="pl-k">from</span> <span class="pl-s1">guidance</span>.<span class="pl-s1">library</span> <span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-s1">one_or_more</span>

<span class="pl-en">@<span class="pl-en">guidance</span>(<span class="pl-s1">stateless</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-c1">True</span>)</span>
<span class="pl-k">def</span> <span class="pl-en">_gen_heading</span>(<span class="pl-s1">lm</span>: <span class="pl-smi">Model</span>):
    <span class="pl-s1">lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">+=</span> <span class="pl-en">select</span>(
        <span class="pl-s1">options</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span>[<span class="pl-en">_gen_text_in_tag</span>(<span class="pl-s">"h1"</span>), <span class="pl-en">_gen_text_in_tag</span>(<span class="pl-s">"h2"</span>), <span class="pl-en">_gen_text_in_tag</span>(<span class="pl-s">"h3"</span>)]
    )
    <span class="pl-s1">lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">+=</span> <span class="pl-s">"<span class="pl-cce">\n</span>"</span>
    <span class="pl-k">return</span> <span class="pl-s1">lm</span>

<span class="pl-en">@<span class="pl-en">guidance</span>(<span class="pl-s1">stateless</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-c1">True</span>)</span>
<span class="pl-k">def</span> <span class="pl-en">_gen_para</span>(<span class="pl-s1">lm</span>: <span class="pl-smi">Model</span>):
    <span class="pl-s1">lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">+=</span> <span class="pl-s">"&lt;p&gt;"</span>
    <span class="pl-s1">lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">+=</span> <span class="pl-en">one_or_more</span>(
        <span class="pl-en">select</span>(
            <span class="pl-s1">options</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span>[
                <span class="pl-en">_gen_text</span>(),
                <span class="pl-en">_gen_text_in_tag</span>(<span class="pl-s">"em"</span>),
                <span class="pl-en">_gen_text_in_tag</span>(<span class="pl-s">"strong"</span>),
                <span class="pl-s">"&lt;br /&gt;"</span>,
            ],
        )
    )
    <span class="pl-s1">lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">+=</span> <span class="pl-s">"&lt;/p&gt;<span class="pl-cce">\n</span>"</span>
    <span class="pl-k">return</span> <span class="pl-s1">lm</span></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">Now, the function to define the body of the HTML itself:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-python notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" dir="auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="@guidance(stateless=True)
def _gen_body(lm: Model):
    lm += &quot;&lt;body&gt;\n&quot;
    lm += one_or_more(select(options=[_gen_heading(), one_or_more(_gen_para())]))
    lm += &quot;&lt;/body&gt;\n&quot;
    return lm"><pre><span class="pl-en">@<span class="pl-en">guidance</span>(<span class="pl-s1">stateless</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-c1">True</span>)</span>
<span class="pl-k">def</span> <span class="pl-en">_gen_body</span>(<span class="pl-s1">lm</span>: <span class="pl-smi">Model</span>):
    <span class="pl-s1">lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">+=</span> <span class="pl-s">"&lt;body&gt;<span class="pl-cce">\n</span>"</span>
    <span class="pl-s1">lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">+=</span> <span class="pl-en">one_or_more</span>(<span class="pl-en">select</span>(<span class="pl-s1">options</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span>[<span class="pl-en">_gen_heading</span>(), <span class="pl-en">one_or_more</span>(<span class="pl-en">_gen_para</span>())]))
    <span class="pl-s1">lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">+=</span> <span class="pl-s">"&lt;/body&gt;<span class="pl-cce">\n</span>"</span>
    <span class="pl-k">return</span> <span class="pl-s1">lm</span></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">Next, we come to the function which generates the complete HTML page.
We add the HTML start tag, then generate the header, then body, and then append the ending HTML tag:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-python notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" dir="auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="@guidance(stateless=True)
def _gen_html(lm: Model):
    lm += &quot;&lt;html&gt;\n&quot;
    lm += _gen_header()
    lm += _gen_body()
    lm += &quot;&lt;/html&gt;\n&quot;
    return lm"><pre><span class="pl-en">@<span class="pl-en">guidance</span>(<span class="pl-s1">stateless</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-c1">True</span>)</span>
<span class="pl-k">def</span> <span class="pl-en">_gen_html</span>(<span class="pl-s1">lm</span>: <span class="pl-smi">Model</span>):
    <span class="pl-s1">lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">+=</span> <span class="pl-s">"&lt;html&gt;<span class="pl-cce">\n</span>"</span>
    <span class="pl-s1">lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">+=</span> <span class="pl-en">_gen_header</span>()
    <span class="pl-s1">lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">+=</span> <span class="pl-en">_gen_body</span>()
    <span class="pl-s1">lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">+=</span> <span class="pl-s">"&lt;/html&gt;<span class="pl-cce">\n</span>"</span>
    <span class="pl-k">return</span> <span class="pl-s1">lm</span></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">Finally, we provide a user-friendly wrapper, which will allow us to:</p>
<ul dir="auto">
<li>Set the temperature of the generation</li>
<li>Capture the generated page from the Model object</li>
</ul>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-python notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" dir="auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="from guidance.library import capture, with_temperature

@guidance(stateless=True)
def make_html(
    lm,
    name: str | None = None,
    *,
    temperature: float = 0.0,
):
    return lm + capture(
        with_temperature(_gen_html(), temperature=temperature),
        name=name,
    )"><pre><span class="pl-k">from</span> <span class="pl-s1">guidance</span>.<span class="pl-s1">library</span> <span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-s1">capture</span>, <span class="pl-s1">with_temperature</span>

<span class="pl-en">@<span class="pl-en">guidance</span>(<span class="pl-s1">stateless</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-c1">True</span>)</span>
<span class="pl-k">def</span> <span class="pl-en">make_html</span>(
    <span class="pl-s1">lm</span>,
    <span class="pl-s1">name</span>: <span class="pl-s1">str</span> <span class="pl-c1">|</span> <span class="pl-c1">None</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-c1">None</span>,
    <span class="pl-c1">*</span>,
    <span class="pl-s1">temperature</span>: <span class="pl-smi">float</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-c1">0.0</span>,
):
    <span class="pl-k">return</span> <span class="pl-s1">lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">+</span> <span class="pl-en">capture</span>(
        <span class="pl-en">with_temperature</span>(<span class="pl-en">_gen_html</span>(), <span class="pl-s1">temperature</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-s1">temperature</span>),
        <span class="pl-s1">name</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-s1">name</span>,
    )</pre></div>
<p dir="auto">Now, use this to generate a simple webpage:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-python notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" dir="auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="lm = phi_lm

with system():
    lm += &quot;You are an expert in HTML&quot;

with user():
    lm += &quot;Create a simple and short web page about your life story.&quot;

with assistant():
    lm += make_html(name=&quot;html_text&quot;, temperature=0.7)"><pre><span class="pl-s1">lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">phi_lm</span>

<span class="pl-k">with</span> <span class="pl-en">system</span>():
    <span class="pl-s1">lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">+=</span> <span class="pl-s">"You are an expert in HTML"</span>

<span class="pl-k">with</span> <span class="pl-en">user</span>():
    <span class="pl-s1">lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">+=</span> <span class="pl-s">"Create a simple and short web page about your life story."</span>

<span class="pl-k">with</span> <span class="pl-en">assistant</span>():
    <span class="pl-s1">lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">+=</span> <span class="pl-en">make_html</span>(<span class="pl-s1">name</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-s">"html_text"</span>, <span class="pl-s1">temperature</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-c1">0.7</span>)</pre></div>
<p dir="auto">When running in a Jupyter Notebook so that the widget is active, we get the following output:</p>
<p dir="auto"><a target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer" href="/guidance-ai/guidance/blob/main/docs/figures/widget_make_html_20250703.png"><img src="/guidance-ai/guidance/raw/main/docs/figures/widget_make_html_20250703.png" alt="Guidance widget showing HTML generation with token fast-forwarding" style="max-width: 100%;"></a></p>
<p dir="auto">Note the varying highlighting of the generation.
This is showing another of Guidance's capabilities: fast-forwarding of tokens.
The constraints imposed by a grammar often mean that some tokens are known in advance.
Guidance doesn't need the model to generate these; instead it can insert them into the generation.
This saves forward passes through the model, and hence reduces GPU usage.
For example, in the above HTML generation, Guidance always knows the last opening tag.
If the last opened tag was <code>&lt;h1&gt;</code> (for example), then as soon as the model generates <code>&lt;/</code>, Guidance can fill in <code>h1&gt;</code> without needing the model to perform a forward pass.</p>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Generating JSON</h3><a id="user-content-generating-json" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Generating JSON" href="#generating-json"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">A JSON schema is actually a context free grammar, and hence it can be used to constrain an LLM using Guidance.
This is a common enough case that Guidance provides special support for it.
A quick sample, based on a Pydantic model:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-python notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" dir="auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="import json
from pydantic import BaseModel, Field

from guidance import json as gen_json

class BloodPressure(BaseModel):
    systolic: int = Field(gt=300, le=400)
    diastolic: int = Field(gt=0, le=20)
    location: str = Field(max_length=50)
    model_config = dict(extra=&quot;forbid&quot;)

lm = phi_lm

with system():
    lm += &quot;You are a doctor taking a patient's blood pressure taken from their arm&quot;

with user():
    lm += &quot;Report the blood pressure&quot;

with assistant():
    lm += gen_json(name=&quot;bp&quot;, schema=BloodPressure)

print(f&quot;{lm['bp']=}&quot;)

# Use Python's JSON library
loaded_json = json.loads(lm[&quot;bp&quot;])
print(json.dumps(loaded_json, indent=4))

# Use Pydantic
result = BloodPressure.model_validate_json(lm[&quot;bp&quot;])
print(result.model_dump_json(indent=8))"><pre><span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-s1">json</span>
<span class="pl-k">from</span> <span class="pl-s1">pydantic</span> <span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-v">BaseModel</span>, <span class="pl-v">Field</span>

<span class="pl-k">from</span> <span class="pl-s1">guidance</span> <span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-s1">json</span> <span class="pl-k">as</span> <span class="pl-s1">gen_json</span>

<span class="pl-k">class</span> <span class="pl-v">BloodPressure</span>(<span class="pl-v">BaseModel</span>):
    <span class="pl-s1">systolic</span>: <span class="pl-smi">int</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-en">Field</span>(<span class="pl-s1">gt</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-c1">300</span>, <span class="pl-s1">le</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-c1">400</span>)
    <span class="pl-s1">diastolic</span>: <span class="pl-smi">int</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-en">Field</span>(<span class="pl-s1">gt</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-c1">0</span>, <span class="pl-s1">le</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-c1">20</span>)
    <span class="pl-s1">location</span>: <span class="pl-smi">str</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-en">Field</span>(<span class="pl-s1">max_length</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-c1">50</span>)
    <span class="pl-s1">model_config</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-en">dict</span>(<span class="pl-s1">extra</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-s">"forbid"</span>)

<span class="pl-s1">lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">phi_lm</span>

<span class="pl-k">with</span> <span class="pl-en">system</span>():
    <span class="pl-s1">lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">+=</span> <span class="pl-s">"You are a doctor taking a patient's blood pressure taken from their arm"</span>

<span class="pl-k">with</span> <span class="pl-en">user</span>():
    <span class="pl-s1">lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">+=</span> <span class="pl-s">"Report the blood pressure"</span>

<span class="pl-k">with</span> <span class="pl-en">assistant</span>():
    <span class="pl-s1">lm</span> <span class="pl-c1">+=</span> <span class="pl-en">gen_json</span>(<span class="pl-s1">name</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-s">"bp"</span>, <span class="pl-s1">schema</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-v">BloodPressure</span>)

<span class="pl-en">print</span>(<span class="pl-s">f"<span class="pl-s1"><span class="pl-kos">{</span><span class="pl-s1">lm</span>[<span class="pl-s">'bp'</span>]<span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-kos">}</span></span>"</span>)

<span class="pl-c"># Use Python's JSON library</span>
<span class="pl-s1">loaded_json</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">json</span>.<span class="pl-c1">loads</span>(<span class="pl-s1">lm</span>[<span class="pl-s">"bp"</span>])
<span class="pl-en">print</span>(<span class="pl-s1">json</span>.<span class="pl-c1">dumps</span>(<span class="pl-s1">loaded_json</span>, <span class="pl-s1">indent</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-c1">4</span>))

<span class="pl-c"># Use Pydantic</span>
<span class="pl-s1">result</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-v">BloodPressure</span>.<span class="pl-c1">model_validate_json</span>(<span class="pl-s1">lm</span>[<span class="pl-s">"bp"</span>])
<span class="pl-en">print</span>(<span class="pl-s1">result</span>.<span class="pl-c1">model_dump_json</span>(<span class="pl-s1">indent</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-c1">8</span>))</pre></div>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="lm['bp']='{&quot;systolic&quot;: 301, &quot;diastolic&quot;: 15, &quot;location&quot;: &quot;arm&quot;}'

    &quot;diastolic&quot;: 15,
    &quot;location&quot;: &quot;arm&quot;

<p dir="auto">Note that the generated blood pressure is not one the model will have seen for a human.
When generating JSON, a substantial number of tokens can often be fast-forwarded, due to the structural constraints imposed by the schema.</p>
